Mr President, vocational training in Euskadi I would say that it is leading, selective, within the State as a whole, with dual training which is a reference and which seeks a model of excellence beyond basic vocational training and, therefore, with many particularities. On the one hand, legislative, because there are competences according to the statute of autonomy and because there was a real decree of transfers, 1441/2010, of transfer of functions in services in the field of vocational training for employment, which orders that things be managed in a certain way and, on the other, by the system of economic concert at the time of financing. This therefore requires that, with regard to the forms of management, the Autonomous Community of the Basque Country can use all of them, from social security contribution subsidies to competitive competition subsidies, etc. It is also possible to combine its reality that, as we have said before, it is quite specific in terms of industrial fabric and in terms of specialization of vocational training, beyond the general structure that may be established by law, and that there is competence to accredit and register the training institutions, as well as the sanctioning power with regard to the obtaining and the possible misuse of the bonuses, or even the functions of programming, management and control in the centres of the Basque Country, beyond the possibility that this group may have centres in other autonomous communities. And we did so with reports from experts who have analysed the subject, some prepared at the initiative of this House such as that of the Geological and Mining Institute of Spain, in which there is clearly talk of a risk of water pollution, air pollution or seismicity. We are talking about issues that, as I said, during these two years, since the previous question was raised, have been being observed and accredited. Therefore, we are no longer faced with elucubrations, but with certainties. And an example that I think is illustrative and that comes from a pioneering country in the practice of fracking as is the United States - where it has produced a series of consequences that have caused many States to have ended up banning it - is the report made by the United States Geological Service that, analyzing the consequences of fracking, has found the proliferation of earthquakes in those places where it has been practiced. Therefore, we are no longer talking about risks, about hypotheses; we are talking about certainties that affect the environment, as has been clear throughout this time, and that affect the safety and health of the population. We also believe that the argument against the economic viability of fracking has lost its full support. That is why, Minister, under these conditions we consider it necessary to bet on another energy model that really respects natural resources and also the population. That is why we advocate, as does the social majority in the Basque Country, that fracking should not be applied and bet on another energy model. What is happening here is that we are not talking about the environment, about what we are talking about here is about other interests, about the interests of certain lobbies, about the interests of economic elites - which is what the Spanish Government is looking at - not watching over the integrity or the interests of the population, no; what it is doing is watching over the economic interests of certain sectors and economic lobbies. That is the question and the explanation of the position of the Spanish Government today. Of all this is aware the society of the whole Spanish state and especially of Euskal Herria and Araba, so they say no to fracking. In addition, it does so on a well-founded and informed position, to which I have previously referred, and it has expressed it in many ways; it has expressed it with massive mobilizations, in consultations, and it has also expressed it in legislative initiatives. I'll mention two of them. Reference has already been made to one of them, which is the one expressed in Navarre, with the adoption of the Anti-Fracking Act, in October 2013, by the hand of the opposition, thus assuming. And the other is currently under way in the Basque Parliament, it is an ILP, with more than 103,000 signatures and which, presumably and always waiting uncertain about what the PNV is doing on this issue, has all the facets of moving forward. These are two clear examples of the will of Basque society and are reflected in both parliaments. The first of these, as my colleague Mr Cuadra has already said, was appealed by delegation of the Government - which is what you do when you do not have sufficient institutional representation to impose your political criterion - and which was clearly confirmed by the Constitutional Court, as it could not otherwise be, in view of the content of your pronouncements, most of them with an absolutely political character, as is the case in these types of issues. With regard to the second, that of the Basque Parliament, which is in the process and comes from the hand of the ILP, you are already in a hurry to try to ensure that it has no effect, that it is not confirmed, as has been known in the press. In view of the matter, we are not only talking about the environment, but, in these terms, we are talking about a question of democracy on a basis that I believe is basic and absolutely logical, namely that those who are going to receive the effects of that practice - in this case, fracking - in the places where they live, are those who decide which natural resources and how they work and extract those resources. It seems logical that those who affect the consequences of this practice are those who, in the first instance and definitively, decide whether they are correct from the point of view of respect for the environment and from the point of view of respect for the health and also of the safety of these people. It seems logical that it is the people who decide whether or not fracking is convenient and that it is the people who decide whether fracking is right or not. In our people, Minister, the position is clear: another energy model is being defended, fracking is clearly not being said, so the only thing left to the Spanish Government is to respect this will. That is why I was telling him that we are talking about the environment, but also about democracy. Finally, I would like to end this speech with a different subject - and I will be brief, Mr President - by denouncing the persistence of political trials against Basque militants today in the Spanish State and the persistence of convictions based on statements that have been denounced as having been obtained with ill-treatment. Because it has enabled them to obtain non-conventional resources that will allow the United States to be fully autonomous in terms of gas through non-conventional gas in a short time, and fully autonomous in terms of non-conventional oil resources as well. And in addition, in a while they can become the world's first gas exporters and the world's first oil exporters. But, more importantly, the availability or not of energy such as gas or oil at a cheaper price than that of its competitors has allowed the United States to allow, over the last few years, not a few industries, precisely European where the price of energy is higher than that of the United States, to move from Europe and just to the United States, with the implications that this has for industrial activity in Europe, for economic activity and above all for employment. When you look for the reasons why in the United States over the past few years there has been a growing process of re-industrialisation as the opposite occurred in Europe in parallel, you have no doubt that one of the most important causes is precisely in the energy price differential, mainly gas. Today gas in the United States is paid at about three and a half dollars, in Europe up to three times and sometimes even more. That is why Europe loses industrial competitiveness in favour of the United States. Thirdly, you were also talking about the need to bet on another energy model - you said it twice - and the question is, what energy model? Because, as far as I know, there are not many European countries that have a mix - a combination of the different technologies with which electricity is obtained in Spain - as diverse as Spain has. In Spain, the generation of electricity contributes nuclear energy, hydropower, wind power, photovoltaics, thermosolars, we have biomass, we have cogeneration, we have coal, we have gas; therefore, there is a certainly diversified mix. I have been able to see that I was thinking of an energy model in which there is a greater weight of renewable energy. In 2014 of the total electricity generation in Spain 43% came from renewable sources. Today in Spain, in 2015, if we are in relation to meeting the targets set by the European Union for 2020, and very particularly as regards the 20% share that renewables would have to have in primary energy, we are above those targets. Therefore, I do not know whether in this energy model you are proposing is to dispense with gas, to dispense with oil, to dispense with coal, to dispense with nuclear energy. And if that's the case, what's the alternative? Because the alternative, logically, would have to be considered if we wanted to change the energy mix in our country. You also mentioned that there were autonomous communities that had opposed different rules and laws to hydraulic fracturing techniques for obtaining non-conventional gas resources. True, there are autonomous communities that have legislated saying: prohibited fracking in this autonomous community. What's the matter? The point is that this is something that is included in the basic legislation of the State, it is not the competence of any autonomous community. It is true that it is the autonomous communities that ultimately have to authorize or not the corresponding permission to carry out this type of prospecting using hydraulic fracturing, provided that this prospecting is to be carried out within a single community, because if it affects two neighbouring communities, which are neighbours, then it is the State that has to authorize it. However, when an operator requests an authorization from an autonomous community to carry out a hydraulic fracture for gas or oil, it has to make the decision on the basis of the basic legislation of the State established for that purpose, because otherwise the operator would go to the courts and would most likely give him the reason. It is true that when an autonomous community - of whatever colour - has legislated banning fracking technique, the Council of Ministers has authorized the President of the Government to go to the Constitutional Court to appeal, but not for the argument you have said. I thought I understood you that as a result of the fact that the party supporting the Government does not have sufficient representation in certain autonomous parliaments, through the delegate of the Government an appeal is made to the Constitutional Court; and I thought I understood you that as the Constitutional does what the Government tells you, then you are right. Man, let me say, with all due respect, that it seems to me an argument of alarming simplicity. The Constitutional Court does what it does to ensure that the requirements, principles and guidelines contained in the Spanish Constitution are complied with. It is true that whenever the Government, the Council of Ministers, has addressed the Constitutional Court to raise the fact that a certain normative initiative of an autonomous community - such as those of hydraulic fracture - is not in accordance with the Constitution, logically, the Constitutional Court has ruled in favour of the Government for what it said before, because this is the basic legislation of the State. Ladies and gentlemen, whether or not the techniques and technologies of hydraulic fracturing are shared has nothing to do with what you proposed from the lobbies. This is a matter of national energy security. You know that the Government has developed a national security strategy where many aspects are included, and one of them is precisely that which has to do with energy, because energy is not only a basic input from any economy, but energy has to be a secure energy from the point of view of its supply, it has to be balanced from the point of view of the different sources with which it relies in order to obtain that energy and it has to be an environmentally sustainable energy, but also economically and financially sustainable. For these three purposes, the national energy security strategy is directed, where no source is excluded for any kind of motivation or ideological consideration. It is true that we have no prejudice, but, yes, what this Government has done in relation to hydraulic fracturing technology is to introduce amendments to the regulations in force in Spain to, on the one hand, increase the requirements required by the Environmental Assessment Act for any type of authorization that is made in relation to this technology and, on the other hand, something that you mentioned in your intervention, establish compensation to the owners of the land where this authorization takes place and also to the autonomous communities and the local authorities closest to the territory where these practices are carried out. In this regard, I would like to mention the recently approved amendment of the Hydrocarbons Sector Act, which sets out these additional considerations, I insist, to increase the guarantee of people and goods and also environmental. ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u166	Urgent inquiry by the Catalan Parliamentary Group into the Government's defence policy aimed at preventing bioterrorism attacks. [[Convergence and Union]] Defends the interpellation Mr. Xuclà.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u167	Thank you very much, Mr President. Minister, three weeks ago the Secretary of State and Head of Cabinet of the President of the Government presented to the Constitutional Commission the report I have and which I show you from the rostrum, the annual national security report. After reading it, my parliamentary group and the spokespersons for the Interior and Defence are greatly concerned about the lack of clear references and a clear and developed strategy on bioterrorism. When we talk about bioterrorism, we don't talk about a threat of the future, we talk about events that have already occurred: the anthrax attack in the United States Capitol, whose disinfection cost millions after the September 11 attacks, and the attacks on Tokyo's subway. There have also been statements by the Government; the only one I can quote right now from the rostrum is a appearance a few months ago by the Secretary of State for Security, Mr Martínez, who informed the Committee on the Interior that in Spain, chatting had been identified - I quote - in a forum near the Islamic State on the use of Ebola as a poison weapon against the United States and against the West. I think it is good to remember that the strategy of defence against bioterrorism in Spain comes from afar. On December 11, 2001, before the terrorist attacks on September 11, at the Royal National Academy of Medicine, then medical colonel Luis Manuel Villalonga Martínez, of the General Health Inspectorate of the Ministry of Defence, in an intervention entitled General Aspects of Biological War, already pointed to the bioterrorism threat. He said - I quote -: There is a biological threat in the field of military, strategic and terrorist operations. I'm finishing the appointment. Ladies and gentlemen, the European States and the United States and Canada have had a clear and in-depth strategy to combat bioterrorism. In 2005, most European States - and I can refer to some of them in more detail, of course France, Germany, Denmark and very active Scandinavian countries - reported that the vaccines they had purchased had become obsolete and that what the French Government was already committed to in a response to last week's French National Assembly: the purchase of third-generation vaccines. But perhaps the most comprehensive project on combating bioterrorism - once again - is the project that was adopted in 2004 in the United States, the Bioshield Act, the biosecurity law to combat chemical, biological, radiological and nuclear weapons. There is a budget, because in the end the policies are defined by specific budgets and this is the last report of the unit of studies of the United States Department of Defense on the millions dedicated, specifically, to combating the various threats linked to bioterrorism: anthrax, smallpox, botulism, nuclear radiation and nerve system attackers. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a reality before which we are concerned because the information we have so far in the national defence strategy, presented three weeks ago by the Secretary of State - number one in the rank of Secretary of State - and head of cabinet of the President of the Government, is non-existent in the matter of bioterrorism, and in a very specific aspect that we discussed in the Constitutional Commission and which I would also like to bring to the fore and debate in this inquiry with the Minister of Defence, I am referring to smallpox and the fight against smallpox. In this country, and throughout the world, people under the age of thirty-five are not vaccinated from smallpox because it was eradicated in the 1980s with an intense campaign of great involvement promoted by the World Health Organization. What information do we have? The information that we have - and that is public - is that the virus exists and that strains are kept in the United States - specifically at the Center for Infectious Diseases in Atlanta, called CDC, as the name of my party, but it has nothing to do with - and also in Russia - at the Vector security laboratory in Koltsovo - and that in the summer of 2004 the United States Government reported that certain vials with that forgotten virus appeared in an undeclassified place - the specific place - of the United States was not reported. On the other hand, advances in biosynthesis have opened up a new pathway for the reappearance of smallpox. The number of people with access and funds, especially non-State organizations such as the Islamic State, is increasing to systematically reconstruct the virus using techniques that currently exist of genetic material synthesis. So far research has been able to reconstruct other dangerous pathogens, such as the polio virus, which has already been artificially and genetically reconstructed by synthesis in a laboratory, and the so-called Spanish flu virus, as well as other pathogens as complex as Mycoplasma bacteria; therefore, rebuilding the smallpox genome is a possibility. Ladies and gentlemen, this is part of the past, but of a past that at the time was terribly deadly. We will easily remember how at the time the Australian Aboriginal population was annihilated, nearly 100 percent annihilated by the smallpox virus and how the mortality rate in the United States, in the indigenous population of the United States, was 80 per cent. Ladies and gentlemen, what is the situation in Spain right now, and in this connection is the policy that we can put forward as a spokesperson for defence, for the interior and for health. What are the facts, ladies and gentlemen, Minister? Spain adopted in 2003 a plan for the preparation of smallpox, entitled Recommendations for action and response to the emergence of a case or an outbreak of smallpox. It was approved by the Interterritorial Council of the National Health System on 15 April 2002 and on 7 February 2003 the Government acquired 2 million vaccines against smallpox. At that time all developed European and American countries were buying, making provision for vaccines. Since there was a great deal of demand for supplies from multiple countries, it was difficult in 2003 to find a vaccine provider and they had to be purchased diluted at 1:10. That was the purchase of 2003, but these vaccines are not current because they expired several years ago. This is the information that we have, provided by the Ministry of Health and which leads us to a very simple conclusion: ladies and gentlemen, today, in 2015, while other countries are providing third-generation vaccines for the fight against bioterrorism - the United States has acquired more than 28 million units -, Spain, waiting for better information that the Minister can provide me on method, approach and prevention and reaction, at the moment does not have the appropriate tools to fight the smallpox virus, and the entire population under thirty-five years is exposed to it. It is important that Spain has legal instruments - and this is another dimension that I would like to put to the Minister and that will certainly be an inspiration for the future motion resulting from the debate -, I do not know whether with a legal rank, with a ministerial rank or with another rank, with instructions that I know in some cases have to be discreet because we are talking about the field of defence, but with total transparency. The United States could illustrate to us, because this law - the law of biosecurity - is extraordinary about the mandate and the follow-up of the funds, about the updating of it in June 2014, absolute transparency of the United States Government before Congress, before the Capitol, to report on the fight against bioterrorism. And disappointment, the clear disappointment of the parliamentary group is the absolute absence of references. A reference, the success of the Ebola Emergency Committee, and it did not cost us anything at the time to congratulate the Government on the good performance and the good reaction in the area of Ebola. But the whole fight against bioterrorism cannot be summarized on pages 129 and 130 of this report without more detailed information. ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u168	Thank you, Mr. Xuclà. I now give the floor to the Minister of Defence.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u169	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, you bring to the House today a matter of remarkable importance, to which precisely because of its gravity we must approach, as you pointed out, with due reservation, not so much because of the condition of the problems linked to it, but precisely because you are talking about the perverse use of certain scientific knowledge in terrorist activity. There has to be, in conclusion, reservation and comedy, beyond the fact that some parts of the matter by which I am asked may be subject to classification, as could be precisely the discovery of certain anti-terrorist agents, whose public awareness might warn those who want to make them of why they do not have to go in order to achieve their objectives. The World Health Organization has always advocated that information in such events be truthful and contrasted to avoid confusion, speculation and collective panic. You are asking me about defence policy in relation to the prevention of bioterrorism attacks. This brings together two risks envisaged in the national security strategy: terrorism and the proliferation of weapons of mass destruction. This union, fortunately unverified, is also in line with the National Defence Directive, which already defines conflict scenarios in which the hybrid is equalising and even shifting to the conventional as to the possibility of the threat, something we are talking about here specifically. Weapons of mass destruction had so far been primarily elements to be taken into account in the assessment of conventional risks to national defence. Indeed, the entire current security system is based to a greater or lesser extent on nuclear deterrence as a point of balance, as a guarantee that conflicts between the permanent members of the United Nations Security Council will not become armed conflicts; deterrence or, as was said a few years ago, the security of mutual destruction, which has also proved to be an effective system for cooling active conflicts such as that of India and Pakistan and deafening other larvae throughout the world. Today the potential relationship between terrorism and weapons of mass destruction is certainly worrying, it is a threat that we have been preparing for for fourteen years. Since the attack you cited at the World Trade Center, the asymmetric threat is perceived more intensely and the interest of non-state actors in biochemistry, nuclear and biological agents is being detected by intelligence. In this context, the United Nations Security Council unanimously adopted Resolution 1540 of 2004, which obliges States, and thus their armed forces, to combat this type of terrorist threat. Immediately at the national level as well as in the defence and security organizations to which we are a party, appropriate steps have been taken to bring the institutions concerned under pressure. It must be, and is, a cross-cutting approach - you mentioned it too - and holistic that, as you will easily understand, runs the Department of National Security of the Presidency of the Government and in which we collaborate the Ministry of the Interior, which has the main competence if we refer to the fight against internal terrorism; the Ministry of Health, responsible for public health and health surveillance and alert, which is very important in this case, and, for our part, the Ministry of Defence in its own responsibilities and with the character of an element of support for emergencies. Our system, which is constantly evolving, is based, like that of all countries in our environment, on the continuous assessment of risk and threat. Without going further and as a sample, the Government is currently working on the extensive network of biological alert laboratories, known as Relab. Returning to the subject of the inquiry, in the field of defence we have developed a specific and incardinated NBQ defence doctrine in NATO. The crisis triggered by the shipment of carbon-containing packages in the United States served as a revulsive to integrate civic-military response capabilities to biological incidents. Thus, the NBQ, nuclear, biological and chemical defence units - surely the best prepared to deal with this type of threat - were integrated, with the capabilities of decontamination and recognition in the system of response to biological incidents. In this doctrine it is the responsibility of the personnel of the military health corps to develop and collaborate in health protection activities in the NBQ environment. In this way, personnel of the military health corps were responsible for training the personnel of the State Security Corps and Forces to manage possible incidents of this kind. On the other hand, these staff and capacities are integrated into the technical reference groups established to manage this type of crisis. The international commitments initially made by Spain in relation to the NBQ defence have resulted in the acquisition of the capacity for decontamination of victims as well as presumptive detection at brigade level and confirmed identification at the level of the NBQ defence regiment, all in relation to the main biological agents, including poxviruses such as smallpox. Currently, in addition to the NBQ regiment, the UME also has advanced capabilities of this type. The following lines of action are included in the defence strategy against bioterrorism in particular by the Armed Forces. Epidemiological warning systems have been developed, which is essential in this case. Major biologic agents with potential bioterrorism use are included in the mandatory reporting disease system, establishing collaborative and information-sharing relationships with civil health for epidemiological surveillance and control of communicable diseases. Capacities have been integrated into health systems, such as the system of decontamination of affected persons, and in this regard the NBQ defence and non-health units have the capacity to decontamination of victims, materials, equipment and vehicles, and sanitary facilities for isolation. Within this doctrine the restriction of movements has also been established as a form of disease control, and health units can be used to establish quarantines and isolation, depending on operational needs and in support of the civil authorities. At the same time - and as you probably know from the dissemination of the recent agreement of the Council of Ministers - the works of improvement of the 22nd plant of the Central Military Hospital of Defense are currently being carried out in order to be able to care in appropriate conditions of biosecurity for individuals affected by extremely infectious agents that could affect the population. As far as training is concerned, the correct assessment of the biological threat and the response to biological incidents are, of course, well known. Therefore, 30% of the curriculum content of the NBQ defense specialist course is dedicated precisely to biological defense. On the other hand, the Armed Forces collaborate with the National Civil Protection System in training activities for voluntary personnel of civil protection groups, as well as local or municipal police, along with personnel of the State Security Forces and Bodies. A good example is the fact that in the past Ebola crisis it was precisely the Armed Forces, and in particular the Earth Army Health Brigade, that were responsible for carrying out training activities for the correct use of personal protective equipment. Communication policies have also been established, as the proper assessment of the threat and knowledge of biological agents always reduces the level of alarm in society. For this reason, personnel of the military health corps have been, and will continue to be, integrated into the communications teams of the Army or the Ministry of Defence in relation to crises related to the use of biological weapons. With regard to interdepartmental integration and coordination, the response to a biological incident involves the integration of capabilities - including, of course, those of nuclear, biological and chemical defence - into the National Crisis Management System, in order to ensure proper and holistic incident management. This integration and coordination is particularly close between the Ministry of Defence and the State Security Forces and Bodies. It serves as an example that personnel of the military health corps, among others, participate actively in the training of personnel of the National Police Corps, Civil Guard, Ertzaintza and Mossos d'Esquadra, and also form autonomous emergency hospital services, such as the Summa 112 of Madrid, the Sacyl de Castilla y León or the SEM of Catalonia or, at the municipal level, the Samur-PC of Madrid. Also for a few years now, extra-hospital emergency health units or the Red Cross have been involved in joint exercises and exercises in which biological defence activities are carried out. Finally, with regard to the generation of response plans, the System for the Monitoring of Diseases of Compulsory Declaration of the Armed Forces is integrated into the National Epidemiological Monitoring Network for the preparation and revision of protocols for diseases included in the system. Going to the heart of his intervention and in relation to the virus of smallpox, eradicated disease whose natural or intentional emergency could have the catastrophic consequences that his lordship pointed out, in all training activities a special emphasis is placed on his study. Early detection of the virus with a correct epidemiological surveillance system will allow us to establish appropriate control measures, including quarantine and isolation, as well as immunoprophylaxis, to reduce the possible transmission of the disease. With regard to the development of new therapeutic products and the maintenance of stocks on the basis of threat analysis, the need for the Defence Pharmacy Centre to produce, within its petition, anti-dotal drugs against chemical agents of war, as well as polyvalent antibiotics against major biological agents has been established. Similarly, potassium iodide is produced for adults and children to deal with other incidents such as radioactives. At the same time, a continuous analysis of new therapeutic products is carried out for possible integration into response plans and is in continuous communication with pharmaceutical companies to monitor the development and application of new products. In the particular case of smallpox virus, the Defense Pharmacy Centre has had a strategic vaccine reserve since 2001. The health authorities established, on the basis of risk analysis, a post-exposure vaccination policy in case of spread of the virus. The honourable Member, in short, the Armed Forces and in particular their NBQ defence units are trained in biological defence, enabling them to detect, decontaminate and prevent the spread of biological warfare and bioterrorism agents in the event of such an event. All of its NBQ defence policies are integrated into NATO's standardization agreements, in the response plans of the Ministry of the Interior and in the protocols designed for this purpose by the Ministry of Health, in all cases with the necessary knowledge at the highest level of scientific current and with the availability of the latest technologies and equipment available in any country in our environment. Thank you very much, Mr President. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u209	We now turn to the validation of Royal Decree-Law 5/2015, of 30 April, of urgent measures regarding the marketing of the rights to exploit audiovisual content of professional football competitions. To present this royal decree-law, the Minister of Education, Culture and Sport has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u210	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to appear before the plenary of this House to request from it the validation of the Royal Decree-Law of urgent measures relating to the marketing of audiovisual rights in professional football competitions. [[The lady]] In the same way as the intervention, I would like to say that it is the wish of the Government, and I obviously extend it to the groups of the House, that this royal decree-law should be the subject of the widest possible consensus in this House and beyond. I will frame the issue very briefly in its social and economic context. I shall begin by pointing out that both amateur and professional football are phenomena of extraordinary social, economic and media relevance in this country, as your ladies and gentlemen know. By brushing the quantitative dimensions of this importance, it is enough to say that the licenses of federated footballers amount to about one million; according to the latest data there are 856,000, in turn grouped into 21,500 federated clubs, and account for 25% of all sports licenses in the country. From the point of view of professional football and its potential as a sporting phenomenon, in these days when we are experiencing the final stages of European club competitions, it would be enough to point out that in the past year, in the competition corresponding to 2013-2014, two of the four semifinalists, the two finalists, obviously also the Champions League champion, were Spanish clubs. That situation, as far as the main club competition is concerned, is also repeated now as regards semifinalists, and hopefully also as regards finalists after this afternoon. From that point of view, it is Europe's first sports power. The second most relevant competition at the club level, the Europa League, also last year had a Spanish champion and, in the competition of the 2014-2015 season we met again with a Spanish club on the way to the final. Apart from this, of course, are the successes of the Spanish Selection that are in everyone's mind. Professional football is therefore an element of singular importance, as I said, not only sports, but also social and economic. The part to which this royal decree-law refers, which is that of the marketing of audiovisual rights, is of extraordinary economic importance, since these audiovisual rights, even in a situation that, as I will describe, does not allow to exhaust all the economic potentials that it could have, comes to represent a very high proportion of the income of professional clubs: between 17%, which is the smallest proportion - in clubs with higher budgets - up to no more than 90% of the income obtained by those clubs. In this context, the regulation, through this royal decree-law of the commercialization of the audiovisual rights of the national competitions, has as objective scope of application the two professional leagues, that is, the leagues of First and Second Division, and the competitions of national scope organized by the Royal Spanish Football Federation, that is, the Cup of his majesty the king and the Super Cup. And as for the type of rights that are regulated, it covers any type of issue, i.e. live or deferred emissions, full or summary emissions, open or coded emissions and free emissions or payment emissions. It does not include this object of treatment - which is not amended in a different regulation - the treatment of radio broadcasts which, as you know, are subject to compensation for the costs that these broadcasts entail for clubs and which are therefore basically under the umbrella of the right to information. With regard to temporary delimitation, commercialization is provided for in the Royal Decree-Law in blocks of three years, i.e. marketing rights cannot last more than three years and, the markets on which they are projected are all markets, both the domestic and international markets in their broadest scope. Why regulation? As you know, up to now the treatment of these audiovisual rights has been based, from a conceptual point of view, on the so-called right of sand with opposition, that is, it was understood that the right was the club in whose premises the element of competition the party was dealing with with with the right of opposition to that right of the club which opposed it, which is the most classical form, and it was almost necessary to speak in terms, at least of the great competitions of the countries where it is most important in these competitions, of the most archaic regime that applies. Indeed, more than this system is this lack of a system, which presents a high number of inefficiencies and poses a high number of problems. In the minds of all your ladies and gentlemen, there are those who have been calling themselves football wars, precisely in terms of different types of litigation and discrepancies in the management of the marketing of these audiovisual rights. What are the reasons for this inefficiency or the problems posed by the system? Firstly, a breakdown of the offer. In the face of a concentrated demand from operators seeking to market these rights, this deterioration in supply, which meant a certain limitation in the collection of resources in national markets, brought with it, above all, a very important limitation of profitability in international markets. Let me give you some very expressive figures on what these limitations entailed. I made a reference a few minutes ago to the power of Spanish clubs in these European competitions, and that power is nothing more than a reflection of the importance of national competition itself. In this context, please bear in mind that with data from the last season 2013-2014, the resources brought together through the sale of audiovisual rights over national competitions were, in the case of Spain, 766 million euros compared to 1,875 million euros brought together by the British competitions, by the Premier League and the Second Division and even 846 million obtained by the Italian Series A which has an appeal, let's say measurable through other metrics, much lower than that of the Spanish competition. There is also a second dimension of inefficiency in this deregulated system which refers to the income imbalance between the different competitors, and which not only has the obvious implication that results from an excessive imbalance from the point of view of the economic survival of the actors themselves, but also has a considerable implication, from a point of view that we could consider strictly sporting, that is, from the competitive balance that must be sought. I referred earlier to the part of the revenue that television rights represent, and I said that in the case of clubs that have less capacity to generate revenue by other channels, they account for 90% of that revenue. But that being the case, it is even more striking that so far, as these revenues were distributed, two clubs that received a larger proportion of them had 33 % of all the revenues generated by the system and, four clubs, 45 % of that total revenue. This undoubtedly has an impact not only, as I say, on the economic balance of the clubs - with the consequences that this has so much with regard to the debts to Finance and Social Security - but also with the very viability of the sports anonymous societies or the clubs basically sports anonymous societies. Thirdly, I have pointed out earlier, this has historically led to a very high degree of conflict over the content of contracts for the assignment of rights. They were individual contracts which established clubs with different operators or with different intermediaries in the operation, which gave rise to a high level of litigation, which in some cases led to spectators not being able to access these broadcasts, these audiovisual rights, which made Spain the only exception in the context of those countries where professional football competitions are most important, and I am referring to England, within the United Kingdom, Germany, France and Italy. Only in Spain, so far, these audiovisual rights were not the subject of centralized marketing. In turn, this centralized marketing, in the case of France and Italy, has been dealt with by regulation; and in the case of Germany and England, by agreement between the parties. What are the objectives of this royal decree-law? However, without the numbering order assuming priority over its importance, the objectives are, first of all, the most obvious, that of ordering the management, operation and control to increase the performance of the system. Secondly, to ensure the viability of the institutions and the competitive balance. Thirdly - and I will insist on this later - we are strongly promoting support for amateur sport. Fourthly, to encourage the development and consolidation of audiovisual markets which, despite having started in Spain with remarkable vigor, precisely because of the lack of adequate discipline in the marketing of these audiovisual rights, have had limited development in Spain - I am naturally referring to pay television -. And also, because it is the function of the Government to promote conditions for the satisfaction of debts and obligations to Finance and Social Security to be met in the best possible way, to improve the economic and financial control mechanisms of clubs and sports companies, which entails a greater guarantee that the revenues of the State Tax Administration Agency and of course the Treasury of Social Security will be aligned. In short, we are going to seek a regularisation and a greater economic balance in professional football, which, like some of the figures I referred to a few minutes ago before your ladies and gentlemen, shows that it is a sector of very considerable economic importance. Finally, from the point of view of European legislation, the system of exploiting the summaries of competitions must be aligned. I shall refer briefly below, ladies and gentlemen, to the regulation adopted by the Royal Decree-Law. From the point of view of ownership, the matter which has been the subject of some kind of confusion on the part of the public as to who it is, I would like to say that it is based on the classic concept of ownership of these rights. A concept that has not been changed in the successive occasions when it has been the subject of specific regulations, neither in the Sports Act of 1990 nor in 1997, has always remained the title and, on the other hand, it has always been operated under the assumption of the ownership of the participating clubs and, indeed, it is the same thing that is done in this royal decree-law, but - this is the new element - understanding that this title of the clubs in order to be able to produce this commercialization, in the sense that the norm establishes it, has to appear the compulsory assignment to the organizing entities of each of the competitions of these limited marketing powers - that is to say - to the aspects that are the own of this regulation, which are those that refer to what happens in the perimeter of the field of play. Therefore, what is beyond that scope logically remains as a right of ownership of each of the clubs. This means that the condition of participation in professional football leagues implies that the participating clubs accept the assignment of that title with regard to the marketing of their audiovisual rights to the benefit of the organizing entity, in this case the National League of Professional Football. It also means that the teams participating in the competitions organized by the Royal Spanish Football Federation, that is, the Cup of His Majesty the King and the Super Cup of Spain also have, as a condition for their participation in this competition, to assign these rights to that federation. Marketing is carried out by establishing a number of general conditions aimed at meeting cleaning, transparency and competition requirements in the way it is carried out and is carried out under two different modalities: exclusive marketing and non-exclusive marketing. It is a question of narrowing down the three-year space to which I referred earlier, and with a configuration through packages of a system in which a public, transparent, competitive and non-discriminatory tender allows such marketing in the most efficient terms. A number of specific rules are added to the Royal Decree-Law to avoid over-concentration, non-competitive concentration, and therefore limit the number of packages to which a bidder can access whether an operator or not. In addition to this exclusive marketing regime, there is also a non-exclusive marketing regime in which more than one or all those wishing to aspire to these rights can participate. Within the standard, it is very important to establish the criteria for the allocation of the quantities obtained. By summarizing a rather complex treatment of the issue in the Royal Decree-Law, I would point out that as far as the division between the First and Second Divisions is concerned, the former will obtain 90% of that income and the latter 10%. How are those revenues distributed between the First and Second Divisions? 50 % of the revenue corresponding to each of the two competitions in the case of the First Division and 70 % in the case of the Second Division - at least 70 % in the case of the second - shall be shared equally among all participants. The remainder, 50 % in the case of the First Division and not more than 30 % in the case of the second, shall be distributed according to two criteria. One, that of the sporting results, which in the case of the First Division will refer to the previous five seasons and, in the case of the Second Division, given the lower stability in the presence of sports clubs and public limited companies participating in the first Division, will only cover the last season and will be taken into account the classification they have obtained in the respective competition. The rest of the variable part of the distribution of these revenues will take into account the social impact of each of the participants who in turn will consider the collection in box office of the stadiums of the clubs, the generation of resources of all kinds, merchandising, transmissions, etc. And what is the purpose of these distribution criteria? That in the face of a current situation where the disproportion between clubs receiving larger amounts and those receiving smaller amounts is the highest of all European leagues, we are moving towards a system in which initially the ratio between clubs receiving more and those receiving less is 4.5 to 1 - we are always talking about the First Division - but to the extent that the revenues obtained by this marketing exceed EUR 1 billion and until they reach EUR 1.5 billion, this ratio will be progressively reduced in such a way that, once those revenues are reached of EUR 1.5 billion, the ratio between the highest and the lowest is 3.5 times to 1. In the meantime, for the next six seasons, a transitional formula will be agreed according to which all clubs will have to perceive at least the amounts they have received in this last season. For the Cup and the Super Cup it will be the Royal Spanish Football Federation that does so, since it is the one who, as far as the end of the Cup is concerned, will obtain all the revenues and distribution criteria, and a formula is provided for it to be the organizer of the competition, that is, the Royal Spanish Football Federation, it will be the one that chooses to give the management to the league, in which case it is guaranteed the greater of two quantities or 1 % of the total of all rights or a closed amount, if it is less than 1 %, 10 million euros. Of course, this refers to the stages prior to the end of the Cup and Super Cup and all revenues correspond to the federation. On this basis of income distribution, it is important how the Royal Decree-Law establishes a number of obligations of beneficiaries to distribute a significant part of these income for the benefit of certain social functions. The most important of these is the 3.5% that has to be committed to a compensation fund, which has been called the Downset Compensation Fund, so that those clubs that lose the category, that is, those that move from First Division to Second Division and those that move from Second Division to Second B, can face the economic imbalance that this loss of the category entails, and there is no repetition of the case that we have so often seen that the loss of the category ends, in the medium and even in the short term, with the disappearance of the club in question. There is a 1% that must be assigned to the Professional Football League for the international promotion of our competitions. This is particularly important because what basically explains the lag in the ability to generate income from our professional football, through the marketing of audiovisual rights in relation to other competitions, is that the international sale of those rights so far has had a very limited capacity. It is about getting that international sale which, as I say, has been very limited so far, I do not say that it achieves what is achieved by the Premier League of England, which is the first, but rather that it comes close to what seems to us to be a rational objective of obtaining revenue from that international sale, which would be around 400 or 500 million euros. 1% has to be given to the Spanish Football Federation for promotion of amateur football, and this also with a minimum guarantee of 10 million for this attention. Finally, there is 1.5 % administered through the Higher Sports Council for Solidarity, from the point of view of support for high-level non-professional sport, job placement of professionals, support for women's football, support for basic football, support for professional associations of football unions, etc. As far as the expected impacts of this standard are concerned, as I have pointed out, the aim is to bring the marketing of audiovisual rights in our competitions closer to the economic potential they can have. We have set reasonable short-term targets of EUR 1 billion, from which, as I said a moment ago, EUR 400 or 500 million must be obtained from the international marketing of these competitions. The aim is also to improve competition and the development of television markets. An important aspect of the rule on which I would like to draw the attention of your honourable Members is that it is envisaged that in the marketing that is carried out, through pay television, it is necessary to make available to all operators on fair terms basic rights of image, transmission, so that the development of pay television that has to do with this better and safer discipline of the marketing of rights benefits all operators. Moreover, it is very important, and I would like to stress this, to the extent that a rule of these characteristics has to improve the economic and financial control of competitions. In line with the International Financial Fair Play provisions of the year 2010 and the regulation of economic control of sports clubs and public limited companies, launched following an agreement between the Higher Sports Council and the Professional Football League in April 2012, changes are made to the Sports Act that strengthen the financial control of public limited companies. In short, they are aimed at ensuring a serious use of economic resources by these companies to safeguard situations such as the current ones, in which high debts to the Public Finance and Social Security entail a risk of disappearance in any event where there are an excessive number of entities in a competitive position with creditors. It also has a positive gender impact as resources are devoted to the promotion of women's football. It has a positive budgetary impact, as the resources to be managed by the Higher Sports Council will allow for the inclusion of high-level athletes in the special scheme of self-employed workers and that 0.5% devoted to the attention of non-aficiency football, to the support of football unions, etc., also has a positive budgetary implication, since so far these were resources that were obtained from the State's General Budgets. In short, it is a matter of simultaneously providing not only greater economic returns that will improve competition and also improve the possibilities for the operation of these clubs, but also of attending to a number of social services such as those I have described in this intervention. The reason for the extraordinary and urgent need of this rule has two fundamental dimensions: the first of them, as you know perfectly well, is that all the interested parties in the matter have repeatedly asked the Government to enact this rule and that is the cause, and that is the second reason, which has to do with urgency, with the fact that after the agreement that was signed for three seasons in the summer of 2012, this season that contract ends and most clubs do not want to continue to maintain individual contracts of the kind that have been made, they understand that there is a situation of insecurity, that situation of insecurity is particularly harmful to enhance the international sale in the sense to which I have referred. For this reason, the whole football sector, apart from the fact that there may be some interests that are not fully satisfied with the content of the rules, are pushing to use this exceptional way of the decree-law. In conclusion, Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to say that the Government has the determined purpose, once this rule has been validated, of facilitating a frank dialogue with those actors who express some major discrepancies with the content of this rule, although in many cases these discrepancies do not actually have so much to do with the content of the rule as with other outstanding issues in other normative frameworks and even fundamentally in the conventional framework, in the regulation that, on the one hand, the collective agreement, and on the other hand, the convention currently in force between the Professional Football League and the Spanish Football Federation, and that this spirit of dialogue and overcoming these difficulties will put it into practice outside the validation of this actual decree-law for which I call for the support of the Chamber groups. Thank you very much, Madam President. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u211	Thank you, Minister. Firstly, for the Joint Group, Mrs Pérez.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u212	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, Minister, the dust that has been lifted by this royal decree gives an idea of the economic and business magnitudes that can be talked about. Professional football, that of the big ones, has long since ceased to be a sport to become, at best, a sport show, but above all in a big business where there are big interests at stake, never better said, and where no one wants to lose their privileges. What seems clear in this totum of alleged grievances is that the Higher Sports Council - and ultimately you, Minister - has acted as usual. For example, the common complaint of all parties is that it has not been dialogued and everyone feels alone. The criteria for the distribution of revenue from the sale of the audiovisual rights of the First and Second Division clubs, in our view, will lead to the rich, with the cooperation of the Government, being even richer and more powerful and to the widening of the differences between them and the rest, which in practice will increase the inequalities between categories. Article 5 of the Royal Decree establishes these criteria for the distribution of income between the First and Second Divisions in a ratio of 90/10. Only by way of example, Minister, will I tell you that in France 65% is allocated to first-rate clubs and 35% to second-rate clubs, which, without going into detail, seems to be a more proportionate distribution and that, above all, it can contribute more to solving the problems of non-payment that professionals may face. On the other hand, once the revenues obtained from marketing have been distributed, Article 6 sets out the mandatory contributions to be made by each entity. Here, too, in our view, the involvement and effort of clubs remain insufficient. Although it is a small step forward in relation to the current situation, we understand that objectives such as the development of amateur football or the contribution to the rest of the sport through the contribution to the Higher Sports Council are far from being met. In short, there is also, through this royal decree, a real stake in football and basic sport in general, which is precisely the one who is most in need of resources, action and political will for its impetus and promotion. Another aspect that we understand more than questionable is that contained in article 7 of the royal decree. Paragraph 1 of this Article provides for the establishment of a monitoring body for the management of audiovisual rights. The truth, ladies and gentlemen, is that there is no need to be a great deal of understanding in this matter to see that this control body is in the hands of the two leading Spanish football clubs and the president of the League of Professional Football, who will also be the one who will clear the possible draws in the votes. These three members are permanent; the rest - two more first and one second - complete the organ and a priori do not seem to have more function than the merely decorative. They will understand that if the control of the management of audiovisual rights will always correspond to the two clubs, which receive much more revenue than the rest for this purpose, and the quality vote to the president of the league, it does not seem to be a very fair or transparent body. I would also like to reflect and set out our position on the controversy that arose in parallel with this royal decree and derived from the tax treatment of footballers' image rights. The BNG has always advocated progress in tax inspection in order to effectively fight avoidance, evasion and tax fraud, especially between high assets and capital income. But, ladies and gentlemen, what we are defending is a global plan and not just specific actions in an election campaign with media objectives, where once the spotlights have been turned off everything remains the same. At this point, and at a time when the social majority of the Spanish state has suffered a lot, it is not acceptable that the income of elite athletes always end up in other countries or tax here as societies in which the partner is the footballer himself, paying less taxes and benefiting from formulas that unfortunately continue to be covered by the current legislation. It is true that high-level sportsmen already had a special regime, approved by the time of the government of the Socialist Party, but it is no less true that the degree of compliance, due to insufficient resources to make the corresponding contributions, was very scarce. What we are talking about is the use of these resources, which are considerable resources - at least EUR 10 million - to make the payment of these contributions to the special scheme of self-employed high-level athletes. Evidently it is opportune, it is always opportune, when resorting to this exceptional procedure of the royal decree-law, whether or not the circumstances of extraordinary and urgent necessity compete and to what extent. I have already made them clear in my speech, but I would like to highlight another aspect which I believe has not been sufficiently appreciated by those who have called into question this extraordinary and urgent need: the unanimity with which all the actors - as I say, regardless of whether they have subsequently expressed or ceased to express discrepancies with some specific aspect of the content - have asked the Government to use precisely this formula of the royal decree-law to produce it. Why? I think it is easy to understand: firstly, because everyone understands that the regime of the absence of a centralised system of sale of these rights significantly harmed their prospects of viability and, secondly, because everyone understands that there is a debt situation and not only with Social Security and with Finance, which, as Mrs Blasco has pointed out, has been substantially reduced, but there are also some debts, the most important, with credit institutions and therefore there is a financially precarious situation that the adoption of such legislation must help to remedy. I would like to conclude this intervention, which is only intended to clarify, stating that the possibilities of reaching consensus to which I referred in my initial intervention do not necessarily have all of them to proceed through the transformation, once validated by the Royal Decree-Law, in its processing. Why? Because, as far as the claims of the Association of Spanish Footballers are concerned, most of them have such a nature that it is made through the conventional instrument, that is, through the collective agreement. Likewise, because some of the claims made by the Royal Spanish Football Federation are perfectly soluble through conventional instruments, that is, through a piece that continues to exist and remains central in the relations between the Royal Spanish Football Federation and the Professional Football League, which is the agreement between these two entities. That is why I would like to reiterate, ladies and gentlemen, that we are working day and night to achieve greater consensus not so much on this rule that, I insist, it is not this rule that makes consensus difficult, but that the resulting balance between the various actors in this system that are going to be affected by the decree-law may be more satisfactory for all. Of course, we understand that the benefit is not only the benefit that the clubs are going to receive, but it is also the benefit of those to whom most of the income of the clubs, which are the footballers, goes. But in addition, and this is the social component that we wanted to highlight, we want there to be an important benefit for sport in general, for amateur sport in particular, and not only for men's and professional football, but for amateur football, for women's football and for high-level sport. Thank you very much, Madam President. [[Applauds]]

First, we have supported the autonomous communities and local entities at a very difficult time from the budgetary point of view, from the financial point of view; we have supported them to pay the outstanding debts to these entities. Thus, a support plan for NGOs was put in place for €3 billion. Secondly, we wanted to safeguard the call for grants from the IRPF. A new legal framework was created to ensure its functioning and also to respond to the competition disputes - Mr Campuzano - which are so often raised in this House. These grants and this call have allowed us to distribute over 630 million euros over the past three years to hundreds of entities and to make possible many programs on which the assistance of thousands of people in our country depends. Thirdly, tax measures have also been promoted. The tax reform introduced by the Government increased the deduction by donations from 25% to 30%; another five points if maintained for at least three years. A non-profit corporation tax exemption route has also been opened. Finally, we hope to be able to continue to deepen fiscal reforms that will help third sector entities. Fourthly, the involvement of third sector entities in shaping the social policy of public administrations has been strengthened. This is channelled through the civil dialogue table with the third sector platform, which is our mechanism of permanent dialogue to improve the effectiveness of social policy. As you can see, we are now giving you special legal consideration as a partner of the Administration. This is therefore the context in which the bill I have the honour to present to you today is framed. I am convinced, as I said at the beginning, that there are so many more things we can share than those that make us have a different vision. I'm going to start by explaining why a third-sector law like the one that's brought up here is necessary. Firstly, we must highlight the reality that exists in our country, with more than 30,000 social entities, whose activity accounts for almost 2% of GDP and which are generating more than 630,000 jobs in Spain. It is therefore a powerful reality, an active reality, a reality that has significance also from the point of view of economic activity. These entities have been present for decades in dealing with situations of social exclusion, they are the vanguard in our country's social progress and progress. They have been the network in the time of difficulties of this crisis, but they are always the network for those who live moments of difficulty even in times of good times. The work of all entities is therefore absolutely key to helping people in vulnerable situations, to promote inclusion and integration and to raise awareness and awareness of our citizens. It has been a long time since this organization called for a regulatory framework at the state level to protect and strengthen this important work that they perform and that I cannot help but recognize and exalt from this rostrum. It has to be a regulatory framework in line with the current reality and the very important evolution that the third sector has had in recent years. It must define its characteristics, its specificities and also its relations with the Administration and its economic sustainability. We regulate within the framework of the competence provided by Article 149.1.1a of the Spanish Constitution, which establishes the exclusive competence of the State in regulating the basic conditions that guarantee the equality of all Spaniards in the exercise of their rights and in the fulfilment of their constitutional duties, and we do so without prejudice to the competences attributed to the autonomous communities in the field of social assistance, we do so expressly without prejudice to those competences. I know that there have been doubts about this in the past and will be raised again this afternoon, but I believe that this has been settled in the draft law which reflects the scope of application of state-level organisations operating in more than one autonomous community or in Ceuta and Melilla. The bill is based on three main pillars. First, to set up a legal framework that recognizes third-sector entities as entities that promote the solidarity of citizens as an expression of participatory democracy that brings new values and puts people's needs at the centre of their actions. Secondly, it is intended to strengthen its capacity as a necessary interlocutor before the General Administration of the State for the design, implementation and monitoring of public policies in the social field. Thirdly, the draft law seeks to define a series of incentives aimed at ensuring the sustainability of these entities. The draft law establishes clear legal outlines and regulations of its own, outlining the nature of the entities. It is absolutely clear from the explanatory statement that its activity cannot and will not in any case dilute the responsibility of the State or interfere with other entities committed to the defence of social interests or the activity of social actors. The third sector is defined below. This definition is also important in order to improve the legal certainty of these entities. It describes it as organizations of a private nature which have emerged from the citizens' or social initiative, under different modalities, which meet criteria of solidarity and social participation, for the purposes of general interest and absence of profit, which promote the recognition and exercise of civil rights, as well as the economic, social or cultural rights of persons and groups who are vulnerable or at risk of social exclusion. After defining them, it sets out and details the essential principles that must govern the activity carried out by these entities. Some are purely organizational, such as having legal personality, being of a private legal nature, lacking profit, ensuring the democratic participation of its members or having an altruistic character. Others relate to actions such as transparency in management, autonomy in decision-making, the promotion of social cohesion or the promotion of equality and non-discrimination. As regards the second of the axes, the draft law strengthens the capacity of the third sector as an interlocutor of the General Administration of the State, as I have insisted, in the design and implementation of public policies in the social field. To this end, it establishes two bodies of participation, the operation of which will be regulated by regulation and which are in fact already in operation. On the one hand, the State Council of Non-Governmental Organizations for Social Action, which is an area of meeting and dialogue of social policies and, on the other hand, the commission referred to earlier for civil dialogue, with the platform of the third sector, commission whose purpose is to institutionalize the collaboration, cooperation and permanent dialogue between the Ministry of Health, Social Services and Equality and that platform, in which the most important entities, organizations and federations of the third sector are framed at national level. This guarantees the real and effective participation of social entities in accordance with the principle of civil dialogue and opens up a forum of permanent dialogue, which is where we are in fact already living. In the third pillar, the draft law contains various forms of promotion and promotion of the third sector, which call for the work of the General Administration of the State in the development of public policies. At the economic level, the text provides that social entities shall enjoy the maximum tax benefits generally recognised in the current system of tax incentives for private participation in activities of general interest, which shall be reviewed whenever necessary in order to improve them. The text provides for the adaptation of public financing systems, always taking into account the prism of budgetary stability and European aid rules. It is also intended to promote the diversification of sources of finance, proposing an improvement of the rules on sponsorship and support for corporate social responsibility. In the area of social awareness, the text provides for other support measures, such as legal instruments that promote the social programmes of the entities or the promotion of the training of persons who carry out their activities and who do their work in this sector. Others pass, for example, to include - they are measures of promotion in the curricula in the different stages of education - contents and references to the solidarity work and the reality of the third sector that help to value it from adolescence, from the younger ones, as an effective way of participation of citizens, or other measures that are also seeking to boost the participation of women and thus the promotion of equality in these entities. In this regard, in the areas of promotion, support and dissemination, the text calls not only on the General Administration of the State, but also on the Autonomous Communities and entities, to establish collaboration, as they are doing, and to strengthen it especially through the signing of conventions. Ultimately, it is about having the capacity to continue to act together, because experience shows and common sense says that when we act together we are much more effective. In addition to these three fundamental pillars which I have outlined briefly and which contain this law, some further progress is being made. The most important, which is striking and which is a very direct mandate, is the adoption of a programme to boost third-sector entities. The Government is obliged to approve this programme of impetus within 12 months of the entry into force of the text. We therefore place very specific duties on ourselves and hope to be able to do so together with the sector. It is a programme that will also have to include concrete measures, which want to implement and develop the provisions for strengthening the entities provided for in the law. From there, once the Government has approved this drive programme for entities, there will be a two-year deadline for a report to be forwarded to this House assessing the effects and consequences of law enforcement. I mean, today we set a framework, today we give a content, today we make legal commitments, but we also commit ourselves to continue working, evaluating and seeing the consequences of the law. We therefore already propose its possible further improvement, in the light of experience. I think that's a good starting point for the law. There were some important issues, after learning about the experience, suffering and difficulties experienced by these entities, which are also incorporated here. For example, it is stipulated that the outstanding payment obligations of the autonomous communities and local entities with the third sector, as a result of agreements concluded in the field of social services, are treated in the same way as those of other suppliers and that, in the aid and in the public subsidies, the regulatory bases of the calls cover the specializations of the third sector in view of the general interest to which they serve and the singularities of their nature and activity. Finally, the text provides for the creation of a public and accessible inventory of entities by the Ministry, following the report of the State Council of Non-Governmental Organizations for Social Action in coordination with the Autonomous Communities. We will have accurate statistical information from social entities, which I believe will be a very useful tool for better knowledge of reality and for more timely management. We are therefore faced with a bill that is new, that does not exist, that comes to cover a vacuum, that comes to satisfy a demand, that comes to respond to an old request, that provides legal certainty, that gives support to the social entities of the third sector and that therefore gives them strength to continue to constitute one of the main social protection networks with which our country has. As I said at the beginning, as always - it is our disposition - there is room for dialogue, improvement and incorporation so that everyone can be represented. In short, both the spirit, the content and the predictions of the law respond to a desire to strengthen a general interest that I have no doubt that we all share. Thank you very much, Madam President. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u259	Thank you very much, Mr Pérez Moya. To defend his amendment, Mr Campuzano has the floor for the Catalan Group. [[Convergence and Union]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-05-13-CD150513.u260	Thank you, Mr. President. We also welcome well-known and friendly representatives of the third sector at the state level. From this point of view the bill is absolutely impeccable, but it consolidates a policy that understands that what is fundamental in the policies of the third sector is the articulation at the state level, and what the government's policy seeks is to strengthen the social and political articulation of the third sector. This, Minister, contrasts with two facts which are very objective and which go beyond a demand from a Catalan political option or from a nationalist political option. The strength of the third sector, the strength of the associative fabric is in the proximity with the citizens, in the proximity to the problems of society, in the articulation from above down, in giving vigour, energy and strength to the social initiatives of the local, the district, the autonomic field, the almost natural sphere where the sector itself is articulated. Government policy tends to ignore this reality and is obsessed with articulation at the state level. In addition, the third sector, if it is an ally of someone in the public sector, is primarily those who exercise competence in social policies, which are autonomous communities and local administrations. That is where that alliance makes sense in those policies that are peculiar to these administrations. The third sector is with these administrations with whom it articulates its relations and collaborations. That is why today we are faced with a state law that places in the centrality of our activity, not in centralism, this important sector without interfering in the competences of the autonomous communities, as the deputy Campuzano pointed out. In the words of those who make up the different platforms and organisations working in the third sector, the regulatory framework that currently supports them is not the right one for the role they play or for the challenges they face.
